The Pahlavi phrase Dtna-i Main6g-1 Khira^, * Opinions  of the Spirit of Wisdom/ is a name applied to sixty-two  enquiries, or series of enquiries, on subjects connected with  the religion of the Mazda-worshippers, made by an anony-  mous wise man and answered by the Spirit of Wisdom. The term 5 ikand«gum^nik Vi^ir, ^ doubt-dispelling expla-  nation,’ is the Pahlavi name applied to a controversial  work by its author. The chief object of the work is to  prove the correctness of the fundamental doctrine of the  Mazda- worshipping religion, that good and evil do not  proceed from the same source, and to show that other  religions, while professing to believe in the unity of crea-  tion, can only account for the origin of evil, either by  degrading the character of the sacred be...

### 40. 3. The Sad Dar.
*lines 1741-1860*

As its name implies the Sad Dar is a treatise on  hundred subjects’ connected with the Zoroastrian religion.  The word dar, literally ‘door, or gate/ being also applied  to the ‘ chapters ’ of a book, and to the ‘ matters, or sub-  jects,’ of which it treats. This work is not a Pahlavi text,  being written in Persian with an admixture of about four  per cent, of Arabic words; it is, however, more quoted  than any other work by the Parsi compilers of the Persian  Riv^yats, or religious ‘tradition...

proportion of quotations that cannot be now identified, or  are no longer extant, is a strong argument in favour of the  work being several centuries older than the oldest manu-  scripts in which it is now found.
